### #101 — @StatsHubCom — 2116588368205119949
Post: https://x.com/StatsHubCom/status/2116588368205119949
TEXT RECOVERY: FULL for text (recovered from `raw/2026-10-31-pm/StatsHubCom.txt`: "The best free stats in the game, and we've got plenty more lined up for this season 🫡", Sat 31 Oct 2026 17:49 GMT; attached image unreadable — whatever product/feature the image shows is image-locked. RESERVE item, dossiered to complete the batch.)
1. WHAT IT IS — A vendor self-claim post: StatsHub calling its own product "the best free stats in the game" with more features promised for the season. Verified against the source in this pass, the claim decomposes into: (a) TRUE as far as it goes — statshub.com's fixtures, live scores, player/team stats, referee stats and its named tools (Prop Hunter, Prop Screener, Player Trends, Team Screener) are indeed free to use, across 120+ soccer leagues, with player stats including xG, shots, shots on target, tackles, fouls and cards, updated in real time; (b) UNVERIFIED superlative — "best" is not measurable as stated, and no comparison set is named; (c) a paid tier exists behind the free claim — the StatsHub Odds API (400+ bookmakers, REST/streaming) is a developer product, so "free" describes the web tools, not the whole business. The account's NFL relevance from earlier corpus work (its Similar Matches product was dossiered in the ON1 window) is not in this post; this window is the soccer product talking.
2. WHERE TO FIND IT — Post: https://x.com/StatsHubCom/status/2116588368205119949 . Primary source: https://www.statshub.com/ (homepage FAQ states the free scope verbatim: "Fixtures, live scores, player and team stats, referee stats, and tools like Prop Hunter, Prop Screener, Player Trends and Team Screener are all free"); tool detail at https://www.statshub.com/player-stats and https://www.statshub.com/team-stats .
3. HOW TO USE IT — Directly usable as a free soccer data source in the browser (no sign-up for the core stats per its FAQ); the Odds API is the developer route (terms/pricing on the site; not evaluated here and no sign-up was made — read-only rule). For GSE's purposes the actionable artifact is the *tool design*: Prop Screener = filter player props by hit rate, odds and recent form; Player Trends = longest current streaks per prop over 3–40 games; Prop Hunter = players whose recent games clear a line, with the live price beside the hit rate. Each is a one-screen spec GSE can mirror for NFL props on nflverse data.
4. WHERE TO USE IT — (i) **GSE Content OS / free-tool funnel**: the StatsHub model — genuinely free, genuinely useful stat tools that tipsters then credit publicly — is the working template for a GSE free NFL prop-screener page feeding the paid packs; it makes the funnel concrete and cheaper than content alone. (ii) Research corpus as the vendor record for the whole StatsHub cluster in this batch. No prediction-engine layer: the data is soccer, and GSE's engine is NFL.
5. CREATIVE ANGLES — (a) The credit economy again, now from the vendor's own mouth: "best free stats" is a claim staked on being *cited by tipsters* — GSE's equivalent asset is one canonical free weekly table (e.g., the pressure-cliff table from this batch's @sfdata9ers dossiers) designed to be quoted with credit. (b) Second-order: StatsHub's free-tools-plus-paid-API split is the same shape as GSE's free-content-plus-paid-packs split; its Odds API is proof the data layer itself can be the paid product if the tools build the audience first — a longer-horizon option for GSE's warehouse (an API for its computed splits) once the audience exists. (c) Cross-sport watch: if StatsHub's free NFL-adjacent products resurface in-season, the sweep should catch them at the product level, not the tips level.
6. RISKS AND LIMITS — Vendor self-claim: the superlative is UNVERIFIED marketing; free-tier scope can change without notice; the site's data provenance (which upstream feeds power its stats) is not disclosed on the pages read, so its numbers should be spot-checked before any republication. The X handle's tips amplification (the rest of this batch) means the brand's "stats" credibility and its tipster distribution are deliberately blurred — cite the tool, never the tips, as the quality evidence.
7. FIRST TEST AND VERDICT — Smallest test for the one transferable idea: spec (one page, no build) an NFL Prop Screener mirror — nflverse inputs, hit-rate-by-line table for receptions/yards/attempts props — and cost it against the Content OS backlog; the vendor post itself needs no test. **Verdict: WATCH** (vendor verified as a real free soccer-stats product with a paid API; the NFL free-tool template is the live takeaway, and the account stays on watch for product-level — not tip-level — NFL output).
